# Invoices (part 1 of 3) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** A Pro workspace can draft an invoice (free lines, or lines pulled from an event's income), issue it under a gap-free per-workspace number, download it as a PDF, email it with the PDF attached, mark it paid (which books the income on the event, net of VAT) and void it, with bank transfer as the only payment method.

**Architecture:** Four tenant tables (`invoice_settings`, `invoices`, `invoice_lines`, `invoice_sends`) that no client role can write: every write is a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC that re-checks `manage_finance` and the Pro entitlement, and a freeze trigger refuses any change to an issued invoice's content from every role. One SQL function computes line and VAT amounts for both the draft save and the issue; a TypeScript port held equal by a shared case table drives the live preview. The PDF is rendered on demand from the immutable `snapshot` taken at issue, with `@react-pdf/renderer`; nothing is stored in a bucket.

**Tech Stack:** Next.js 16.3.8 App Router (Server Actions, route handlers), React 19.2.8, Supabase Postgres (RLS, `SECURITY DEFINER` RPCs, triggers), `@react-pdf/renderer` (new, pinned exact), Brevo HTTP API via `lib/email/send.ts`, Vitest 4, Playwright, psql suites under `supabase/tests/`.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-invoices-design.md` (founder-approved 2026-10-20; the source of truth).
**Ledger:** `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-20-invoices/progress.md` (`.superpowers` is gitignored; add the ledger with `git add -f`).

## Global Constraints

- Branch `invoicing`, worktree `.claude/worktrees/invoicing`, off `launch-hardening`. Its PR stacks after the Phase 10 PR. Rebase onto `launch-hardening` after each Phase 10 task lands (`git pull --rebase` is for this branch's own remote; the stack rebase is `git rebase origin/launch-hardening`).
- New migrations take timestamps after the newest migration on `launch-hardening` when written. Today that is `20261105000000_no_self_service_email_change.sql`, so this plan uses `20261106000000` … `20261106006000`. A rebase that puts one of ours before a Phase 10 migration renames ours forward (and the plan's names with it).
- **Never** run `pnpm build` or `pnpm start`, and never point anything at a hosted Supabase project.
- The local Supabase stack and port 3000 are **shared** with the `launch-hardening` session and with a dev server the founder uses. Before any `db reset`, psql suite, integration run or e2e run: check for other sessions' `node`/`playwright`/`psql` processes, then claim the stack by messaging the `launch-hardening` session naming this branch's migration set (a reset to this branch replaces Phase 10's schema until they reset back), and release it when done. One heavy job at a time across both branches. psql is not on PATH: `docker exec supabase_db_getsetpaid psql -U postgres ...`.
- The Task 1 spike runs its own dev server on **port 3100**, never 3000.
- Never start ClamAV or any extra container locally (the Docker VM is capped at 2 GB).
- e2e: run specs in groups, each `npx playwright test <files> --workers=1`. A worker death `0xC0000142`, `Target crashed`, or `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` is the machine, not the product; re-run that group, never add retries to config. If a run's shell is reaped, stop its children by PID.
- The five gates before a task is done: `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm test:rls`, `pnpm test:e2e` (the e2e gate is run in groups as above; Tasks 1–13 run the groups their change can reach, Task 14 and Task 15 run all of it). After adding or renaming a route, run `npx next typegen` before `pnpm typecheck`.
- psql: `npx supabase db reset --local` then `bash supabase/tests/run-rls-tests.sh` (it refuses to run twice without a reset). While iterating on one task, run the invoices suite alone (command in Task 3, Step 8); before the task's commit, reset and run the whole script once.
- **Every assertion in `supabase/tests/rls_invoices.sql` is one line that begins `select inv.val(`.** Its `run_suite` pin is therefore `grep -c '^select inv.val(' supabase/tests/rls_invoices.sql`, and a task that adds assertions moves the pin to that number in the same commit. A new assertion in any other suite moves that suite's pin.
- TDD for every control: write the failing test (psql assertion, Vitest or Playwright, whichever reaches the layer), see it fail for the stated reason, implement, see it pass, then a **mutant** that reverts the control and turns the test red. Record each mutant (what was reverted, which assertion went red) in the task report.
- Migrations are additive: never edit an applied one. A function that already exists is rebuilt from its live `pg_get_functiondef`, diffed against its latest migration first (`docker exec supabase_db_getsetpaid psql -U postgres -tAc "select pg_get_functiondef('<schema.fn>'::regproc)"`), with only the stated lines added.
- Every `SECURITY DEFINER` function sets `search_path = ''`, schema-qualifies everything, and ends with `revoke all on function … from public, anon, authenticated, service_role;` followed by exactly the grants the task names.
- UI: `DESIGN.md` tokens only (no literal hex), square corners, no shadows or gradients, Source Serif 4, `tabular-nums` on every number. Money is minor units (`bigint` in SQL, `number` in TS) and is shown only through `formatGbp` (`lib/format/money.ts`); dates through `formatDateOnly`/`formatDate` (`lib/format/date.ts`). Every new page has a `loading.tsx`. Every form echoes its submitted values on refusal (`echoedValues`, `lib/forms.ts`).
- Commit and push after every change, one logical change per commit (`git pull --rebase` before `git push`). Every commit message ends with exactly:
  `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`
- Founder questions go to `.superpowers/sdd/plan-the-whole-build-synthetic-thompson/DECISIONS-QUEUE.md` (tracked, `git add -f`) with a default that is implemented. This plan uses **Q248–Q250** (Task 15). The Stripe platform-fee rate is part 2's question, not this plan's.
- Never commit secrets, `.env` files, Supabase service-role keys, the Brevo API key, or the contents of private buckets. Never print `BREVO_API_KEY`.
- Never use bare `git stash`.

## Decisions this plan takes where the spec is silent or meets the codebase

Each is recorded in the task that implements it and reported at the end. Q-numbered ones go to the decisions queue in Task 15.

1. **No client write grants on any invoice table.** The spec allows "table UPDATE only on `draft` rows"; this plan grants `authenticated` SELECT only and routes every write (including draft saves) through RPCs. Stricter, removes the line-DELETE audit gap, and keeps the four tables out of the CRM audit-coverage census (which counts only client-writable tables).
2. **`recipient_address` is a column on the invoice.** HMRC requires the customer's address and no CRM record has an address column (`companies` has none, `contacts` has `location`). The user types it on the draft; issuing requires it.
3. **A draft may have no recipient.** "Invoice these lines" creates a draft "with the recipient left for the user to pick". The check is "at most one recipient" on a draft, "exactly one" once issued.
4. **`paid_on date`, not `paid_at`.** The RPC takes a date (`p_paid_on`), so the column is a date; it also dates the income entry it creates.
5. **"The remaining lines" means lines with no `financial_entry_id`.** A pulled line whose entry is already `actual` (or deleted) is neither flipped nor booked again, so mark-paid never double-counts income.
6. **Mark paid on an invoice whose event has been soft-deleted touches no finance.** A deleted event's finance is read-only everywhere else in the product.
7. **Contact erasure (Q249).** No code path erases a contact today: a contact's data-subject request is routed to the workspace, which soft-deletes the contact, and a soft delete is reversible and is also how a merge retires a duplicate. So drafts are **not** soft-deleted when their recipient is; `issue_invoice` refuses a deleted recipient (`invoice_recipient_deleted`) and the draft stays editable. Issued snapshots are never touched. The spec's "soft-deletes drafts" is queued as Q249 with this default.
8. **Retention (Q250).** All four tables are classified `cascades` in `private.retention_subjects()`: they live exactly as long as the workspace, like `financial_entries` in practice, and an issued invoice is never deleted by its workspace (the freeze trigger refuses it). Classifying `invoices` as `retained_with_workspace` would require a new published version of `/legal/data-retention` (rls_phase8_retention.sql RL4 binds that class to the page), which waits for counsel; queued as Q250.
9. **The bank-change email goes through the existing notice queue** (an audit row, a `service_role` resolver, the `private.notification_deliveries` ledger, the delivery tick), like every other notification since the 2026-09-30 ruling. It is not sent synchronously from the save.
10. **Draft saves and draft deletes are not audited.** The spec's audit list is issue, send, mark paid, void, settings change and PDF download; drafts are working copies.
11. **Numbers never truncate.** `lpad(n, 4, '0')` truncates above 9999 (`lpad('12345', 4, '0') = '1234'`), which would duplicate numbers. Below 10 000 the number is zero-padded to four digits; from 10 000 it is printed in full.
12. **The issue date is the UK-local date** (`(now() at time zone 'Europe/London')::date`), not the UTC date.
13. **The freeze trigger is exercised as `postgres`.** `service_role` holds no write privilege on these tables at all (asserted), so the trigger is proved against `postgres`, which owns the tables and bypasses RLS; a freeze that holds against it holds against `service_role`.
14. **"Invoice these lines" is its own section directly under the finance panel**, rendered only when the caller can write finance; the panel above it already explains a read-only workspace.
15. **VAT number format** is `^(GB|XI)([0-9]{9}|[0-9]{12}|GD[0-9]{3}|HA[0-9]{3})$` after the form strips spaces and upper-cases.

### Task 1: Spike — `@react-pdf/renderer` in the Next.js server runtime, and a Brevo send with a PDF attachment

The spec's build-order step 1: if either half fails, **stop**, write the failure into the decisions queue as Q248's addendum with the measured error, and do not start Task 2.

**Files:**
- Modify: `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml` (the dependency)
- Create: `lib/invoices/pdf/fonts/SourceSerif4-Regular.ttf`, `lib/invoices/pdf/fonts/SourceSerif4-Bold.ttf`, `lib/invoices/pdf/fonts/OFL.txt`
- Modify: `lib/email/send.ts`
- Test: `tests/unit/email/send.test.ts`
- Temporary, never committed: `app/api/spike-invoice-pdf/route.ts`
- Possibly modify: `next.config.ts` (only if Step 4 needs it)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `SendEmailInput.attachments?: readonly EmailAttachment[]` where `export interface EmailAttachment { name: string; contentBase64: string }`; Brevo receives `attachment: [{ name, content }]`.
- Produces: the two font files at the paths above, consumed by Task 10's `lib/invoices/pdf/render.ts`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Add the dependency, pinned exact**

```bash
pnpm add @react-pdf/renderer@4.3.0
pnpm audit --audit-level high
```
Expected: `package.json` shows `"@react-pdf/renderer": "4.3.0"` (no caret; if pnpm wrote `^4.3.0`, edit it to `4.3.0` and re-run `pnpm install`). `pnpm audit` exits 0 (CI runs the same check). If audit fails on this package, stop and record it.

- [ ] **Step 2: Fetch the Source Serif 4 static TTFs (OFL)**

```bash
mkdir -p "$TEMP/ss4" lib/invoices/pdf/fonts
gh release view 4.005R -R adobe-fonts/source-serif --json assets --jq '.assets[].name'
gh release download 4.005R -R adobe-fonts/source-serif -p 'source-serif-4.005_Desktop.zip' -D "$TEMP/ss4"
unzip -o "$TEMP/ss4/source-serif-4.005_Desktop.zip" -d "$TEMP/ss4/x"
find "$TEMP/ss4/x" -name 'SourceSerif4-Regular.ttf' -o -name 'SourceSerif4-Bold.ttf' -o -name 'LICENSE.md' -o -name 'OFL.txt'
```
Copy `SourceSerif4-Regular.ttf` and `SourceSerif4-Bold.ttf` (the static, non-variable `TTF/` instances) into `lib/invoices/pdf/fonts/`, and the licence text into `lib/invoices/pdf/fonts/OFL.txt`. If the asset name listed by the first command differs, download the asset whose name contains `Desktop`. Verify: `file lib/invoices/pdf/fonts/*.ttf` reports `TrueType Font data` for both.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the temporary spike route (do not commit it)**

```tsx
// app/api/spike-invoice-pdf/route.ts -- TEMPORARY (Task 1 spike). Deleted in Step 9.
import path from "node:path";
import { Document, Font, Page, Text, View, renderToBuffer } from "@react-pdf/renderer";

export const runtime = "nodejs";

const FONTS = path.join(process.cwd(), "lib", "invoices", "pdf", "fonts");
Font.register({
  family: "Source Serif 4",
  fonts: [
    { src: path.join(FONTS, "SourceSerif4-Regular.ttf"), fontWeight: 400 },
    { src: path.join(FONTS, "SourceSerif4-Bold.ttf"), fontWeight: 700 },
  ],
});

export async function GET(): Promise<Response> {
  const lines = Array.from({ length: 40 }, (_, i) => `Line ${i + 1} — <script>alert(1)</script> £1,234.56`);
  const buffer = await renderToBuffer(
    <Document>
      <Page size="A4" style={{ fontFamily: "Source Serif 4", padding: 40, fontSize: 10 }}>
        <Text style={{ fontWeight: 700, fontSize: 18 }}>INV-0001</Text>
        <View>{lines.map((l) => <Text key={l}>{l}</Text>)}</View>
      </Page>
    </Document>,
  );
  return new Response(new Uint8Array(buffer), {
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/pdf", "Content-Disposition": 'attachment; filename="INV-0001.pdf"' },
  });
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Prove it runs under `next dev` (port 3100)**

Check nothing listens on 3100 (`netstat -ano | grep ':3100'` is empty), then:
```bash
node scripts/dev-env.mjs && npx next dev -p 3100   # in the background; wait for "Ready"
curl -s -o "$TEMP/spike.pdf" -w '%{http_code} %{content_type}\n' http://localhost:3100/api/spike-invoice-pdf
head -c 5 "$TEMP/spike.pdf"; echo; wc -c < "$TEMP/spike.pdf"
```
Expected: `200 application/pdf`, the first five bytes `%PDF-`, size above 10 000 bytes (an embedded font). Open the file and confirm the text renders in Source Serif 4 and `<script>` appears as literal text.
If the route fails with a bundling error (e.g. `Module not found` or `fontkit`/`yoga` resolution), add to `next.config.ts` inside `nextConfig`:
```ts
  serverExternalPackages: ["@react-pdf/renderer"],
```
restart the dev server and retry once. If it still fails, **stop** (see the task header). Stop the dev server by PID when done.

- [ ] **Step 7: Implement attachments in `lib/email/send.ts`**

Add after the `EmailAddress` interface:
```ts
/** A file sent with the message. Brevo takes base64 content and a file name;
 *  the name is the caller's, so it must never be user-supplied text. */
export interface EmailAttachment {
  name: string;
  contentBase64: string;
}
```
Add to `SendEmailInput` (after `replyTo`):
```ts
  /** Files attached to the message (an invoice PDF). Omitted when empty. */
  attachments?: readonly EmailAttachment[];
```
In the `JSON.stringify({...})` body, after the `replyTo` spread:
```ts
        ...(input.attachments && input.attachments.length > 0
          ? { attachment: input.attachments.map((a) => ({ name: a.name, content: a.contentBase64 })) }
          : {}),
```
Run: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/unit/email/send.test.ts` — Expected: PASS.

```bash
cat "$TEMP/brevo.json"
```

### Task 2: The pure invoice logic — arithmetic, numbering, snapshot

**Files:**
- Create: `lib/invoices/arithmetic.ts`, `lib/invoices/snapshot.ts`, `lib/invoices/status.ts`
- Create: `tests/unit/invoices/arithmetic-cases.ts`, `tests/unit/invoices/arithmetic.test.ts`, `tests/unit/invoices/snapshot.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces (`lib/invoices/arithmetic.ts`):
  - `export const VAT_RATES: readonly ["standard_20","reduced_5","zero","exempt","none"]`, `export type VatRate`, `export const VAT_RATE_LABELS: Record<VatRate,string>`, `export function isVatRate(v: unknown): v is VatRate`
  - `export function parseQuantity(raw: string): bigint | null` (hundredths; `"2.5"` → `250n`)
  - `export function formatQuantity(hundredths: bigint): string` (`250n` → `"2.50"`)
  - `export interface LineAmounts { netMinor: number; vatMinor: number }`
  - `export function lineAmounts(quantityHundredths: bigint, unitPriceMinor: number, rate: VatRate): LineAmounts` (throws `RangeError` above `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`)
  - `export function invoiceTotals(lines: readonly LineAmounts[]): { netMinor: number; vatMinor: number; grossMinor: number }`
  - `export function formatInvoiceNumber(prefix: string, n: number): string`
- Produces (`lib/invoices/status.ts`): `export const INVOICE_STATUSES = ["draft","issued","paid","void"] as const`, `export type InvoiceStatus`, `export const INVOICE_STATUS_LABELS: Record<InvoiceStatus,string>`, `export function isInvoiceStatus(v: unknown): v is InvoiceStatus`
- Produces (`lib/invoices/snapshot.ts`): `InvoiceSnapshot`, `SnapshotLine`, `export function parseSnapshot(value: unknown): InvoiceSnapshot | null`
- Produces (`tests/unit/invoices/arithmetic-cases.ts`): `export const ARITHMETIC_CASES: readonly ArithmeticCase[]` with `interface ArithmeticCase { quantity: string; unitPriceMinor: number; rate: VatRate; netMinor: number; vatMinor: number }` — Task 3 binds the SQL suite's copy to this one.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the shared case table**

```ts
// tests/unit/invoices/arithmetic-cases.ts
// THE ONE CASE TABLE for line and VAT arithmetic. The TypeScript port is
// tested against it here; the SQL function is tested against a copy in
// supabase/tests/rls_invoices.sql, and arithmetic-cases-match-sql.test.ts
// (Task 3) fails if the two copies differ by one row. Rule: per line,
// net = round_half_up(quantity × unit price), vat = round_half_up(net × rate).
import type { VatRate } from "@/lib/invoices/arithmetic";

export interface ArithmeticCase {
  quantity: string;
  unitPriceMinor: number;
  rate: VatRate;
  netMinor: number;
  vatMinor: number;
}

export const ARITHMETIC_CASES: readonly ArithmeticCase[] = [
  { quantity: "1.00", unitPriceMinor: 10000, rate: "standard_20", netMinor: 10000, vatMinor: 2000 },
  { quantity: "2.50", unitPriceMinor: 333, rate: "standard_20", netMinor: 833, vatMinor: 167 },
  { quantity: "0.01", unitPriceMinor: 1, rate: "standard_20", netMinor: 0, vatMinor: 0 },
  { quantity: "0.50", unitPriceMinor: 1, rate: "standard_20", netMinor: 1, vatMinor: 0 },
  { quantity: "3.00", unitPriceMinor: 1999, rate: "reduced_5", netMinor: 5997, vatMinor: 300 },
  { quantity: "1.00", unitPriceMinor: 250, rate: "standard_20", netMinor: 250, vatMinor: 50 },
  { quantity: "1.00", unitPriceMinor: 2, rate: "reduced_5", netMinor: 2, vatMinor: 0 },
  { quantity: "1.00", unitPriceMinor: 10, rate: "reduced_5", netMinor: 10, vatMinor: 1 },
  { quantity: "7.25", unitPriceMinor: 100, rate: "zero", netMinor: 725, vatMinor: 0 },
  { quantity: "1.00", unitPriceMinor: 12345, rate: "exempt", netMinor: 12345, vatMinor: 0 },
  { quantity: "1.00", unitPriceMinor: 12345, rate: "none", netMinor: 12345, vatMinor: 0 },
  { quantity: "12.34", unitPriceMinor: 5678, rate: "standard_20", netMinor: 70067, vatMinor: 14013 },
];
```

```ts
describe("lineAmounts over the shared case table", () => {
  it.each(ARITHMETIC_CASES)("$quantity × $unitPriceMinor at $rate", (c) => {
    const q = parseQuantity(c.quantity);
    expect(q).not.toBeNull();
    expect(lineAmounts(q as bigint, c.unitPriceMinor, c.rate)).toEqual({

  it("refuses a product past Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER rather than rounding it", () => {
    expect(() => lineAmounts(999999999999n, Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, "none")).toThrow(RangeError);
  });

    expect(parseQuantity("2.5")).toBe(250n);
    expect(parseQuantity(" 12.34 ")).toBe(1234n);
    expect(parseQuantity("3")).toBe(300n);
  });
  it("refuses zero, negatives, three decimals, more than ten integer digits and junk", () => {
    for (const raw of ["0", "0.00", "-1", "1.005", "12345678901", "1e3", "", "abc", "1,5"]) {
      expect(parseQuantity(raw), raw).toBeNull();

  });
  it("formats hundredths back to two decimals", () => {
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement `lib/invoices/arithmetic.ts`**

```ts
// Line and VAT arithmetic for invoices -- the TypeScript port of
// private.invoice_line_amounts() (20261106000000). The DATABASE is the source
// of the numbers on an invoice; this port exists for the editor's live
// preview, and tests/unit/invoices/arithmetic-cases.ts holds both to one case
// table. Exact integer arithmetic throughout (BigInt), never floats.
//
// Rule (spec § Arithmetic): net = round_half_up(quantity × unit_price_minor),
// vat = round_half_up(net × rate). Every value here is non-negative, so
// "half up" and Postgres's round(numeric) (half away from zero) agree.

export const VAT_RATES = ["standard_20", "reduced_5", "zero", "exempt", "none"] as const;
export type VatRate = (typeof VAT_RATES)[number];

export const VAT_RATE_LABELS: Record<VatRate, string> = {
  standard_20: "20% (standard)",
  reduced_5: "5% (reduced)",
  zero: "0% (zero-rated)",
  exempt: "Exempt",
  none: "No VAT",
};

const BASIS_POINTS: Record<VatRate, bigint> = {
  standard_20: 2000n,
  reduced_5: 500n,
  zero: 0n,
  exempt: 0n,
  none: 0n,
};

export function isVatRate(value: unknown): value is VatRate {
  return typeof value === "string" && (VAT_RATES as readonly string[]).includes(value);
}

/** A positive quantity with at most two decimals and ten integer digits
 *  (the column is numeric(12,2)), as hundredths; null otherwise. */
export function parseQuantity(raw: string): bigint | null {
  const match = /^(\d{1,10})(?:\.(\d{1,2}))?$/.exec(raw.trim());
  if (!match) return null;
  const hundredths = BigInt(match[1]) * 100n + BigInt((match[2] ?? "").padEnd(2, "0"));
  return hundredths > 0n ? hundredths : null;
}

export function formatQuantity(hundredths: bigint): string {
  return `${hundredths / 100n}.${String(hundredths % 100n).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}

/** round_half_up(n / d) for n >= 0, d > 0. */
function divideHalfUp(n: bigint, d: bigint): bigint {
  return (2n * n + d) / (2n * d);
}

const MAX_SAFE = BigInt(Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER);

export interface LineAmounts {
  netMinor: number;
  vatMinor: number;
}

export function lineAmounts(quantityHundredths: bigint, unitPriceMinor: number, rate: VatRate): LineAmounts {
  const net = divideHalfUp(quantityHundredths * BigInt(unitPriceMinor), 100n);
  const vat = divideHalfUp(net * BASIS_POINTS[rate], 10000n);
  if (net + vat > MAX_SAFE) throw new RangeError("invoice line amount is too large");
  return { netMinor: Number(net), vatMinor: Number(vat) };
}

export function invoiceTotals(lines: readonly LineAmounts[]): {
  netMinor: number;
  vatMinor: number;
  grossMinor: number;
} {
  let net = 0;
  let vat = 0;
  for (const line of lines) {
    net += line.netMinor;
    vat += line.vatMinor;
  }
  return { netMinor: net, vatMinor: vat, grossMinor: net + vat };
}

/** prefix + the sequence number, zero-padded to four digits below 10 000 and
 *  printed in full from there -- lpad() would truncate (decision 11). The SQL
 *  twin is private.format_invoice_number(). */
export function formatInvoiceNumber(prefix: string, n: number): string {
  return prefix + (n < 10000 ? String(n).padStart(4, "0") : String(n));
}
```
Run: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/unit/invoices/arithmetic.test.ts` — Expected: PASS.

```ts
// lib/invoices/status.ts
export const INVOICE_STATUSES = ["draft", "issued", "paid", "void"] as const;
export type InvoiceStatus = (typeof INVOICE_STATUSES)[number];
export const INVOICE_STATUS_LABELS: Record<InvoiceStatus, string> = {
  draft: "Draft",
  issued: "Issued",
  paid: "Paid",
  void: "Void",
};
export function isInvoiceStatus(value: unknown): value is InvoiceStatus {
  return typeof value === "string" && (INVOICE_STATUSES as readonly string[]).includes(value);
}
```

```ts
// lib/invoices/snapshot.ts
// The frozen copy private.build_invoice_snapshot() takes at issue
// (20261106000000). The PDF and the issued view render ONLY from this, and
// it is read back from jsonb, so it is parsed -- never cast. A malformed
// snapshot is null, and the caller renders an error rather than half an
// invoice. Number shape matches invoices.number's CHECK, which is what lets
// the PDF filename carry it (spec § PDF).
import { isVatRate, type VatRate } from "@/lib/invoices/arithmetic";

const NUMBER = /^[A-Z0-9-]{0,12}[0-9]{4,10}$/;
const DATE = /^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/;

export interface SnapshotLine {
  position: number;
  description: string;
  quantity: string;
  unitPriceMinor: number;
  vatRate: VatRate;
  netMinor: number;
  vatMinor: number;
}

export interface InvoiceSnapshot {
  number: string;
  issueDate: string;
  supplyDate: string;
  dueDate: string;
  currency: "GBP";
  vatRegistered: boolean;
  seller: {
    name: string;
    address: string;
    vatNumber: string | null;
    bank: { accountName: string; sortCode: string; accountNumber: string } | null;
  };
  recipient: { kind: "company" | "contact"; name: string; address: string; email: string | null };
  lines: SnapshotLine[];
  totals: { netMinor: number; vatMinor: number; grossMinor: number };
  notes: string | null;
}

type Obj = Record<string, unknown>;
const isObj = (v: unknown): v is Obj => typeof v === "object" && v !== null && !Array.isArray(v);
const str = (v: unknown): string | null => (typeof v === "string" ? v : null);
const int = (v: unknown): number | null =>
  typeof v === "number" && Number.isSafeInteger(v) && v >= 0 ? v : null;

function line(v: unknown): SnapshotLine | null {
  if (!isObj(v)) return null;
  const position = int(v.position);
  const description = str(v.description);
  const quantity = str(v.quantity);
  const unitPriceMinor = int(v.unit_price_minor);
  const netMinor = int(v.net_minor);
  const vatMinor = int(v.vat_minor);
  if (position === null || !description || !quantity || unitPriceMinor === null) return null;
  if (netMinor === null || vatMinor === null || !isVatRate(v.vat_rate)) return null;
  return { position, description, quantity, unitPriceMinor, vatRate: v.vat_rate, netMinor, vatMinor };
}

export function parseSnapshot(value: unknown): InvoiceSnapshot | null {
  if (!isObj(value) || value.version !== 1 || value.currency !== "GBP") return null;
  const number = str(value.number);
  const issueDate = str(value.issue_date);
  const supplyDate = str(value.supply_date);
  const dueDate = str(value.due_date);
  if (!number || !NUMBER.test(number)) return null;
  if (!issueDate || !supplyDate || !dueDate || ![issueDate, supplyDate, dueDate].every((d) => DATE.test(d))) {
    return null;
  }
  const seller = value.seller;
  const recipient = value.recipient;
  const totals = value.totals;
  if (!isObj(seller) || !isObj(recipient) || !isObj(totals) || !Array.isArray(value.lines)) return null;

  const sellerName = str(seller.name);
  const sellerAddress = str(seller.address);
  const vatNumber = seller.vat_number === null ? null : str(seller.vat_number);
  if (!sellerName || !sellerAddress || (seller.vat_number !== null && !vatNumber)) return null;
  let bank: InvoiceSnapshot["seller"]["bank"] = null;
  if (seller.bank !== null) {
    if (!isObj(seller.bank)) return null;
    const accountName = str(seller.bank.account_name);
    const sortCode = str(seller.bank.sort_code);
    const accountNumber = str(seller.bank.account_number);
    if (!accountName || !sortCode || !accountNumber) return null;
    bank = { accountName, sortCode, accountNumber };
  }

  const kind = recipient.kind;
  const recipientName = str(recipient.name);
  const recipientAddress = str(recipient.address);
  const email = recipient.email === null ? null : str(recipient.email);
  if ((kind !== "company" && kind !== "contact") || !recipientName || !recipientAddress) return null;

  const lines: SnapshotLine[] = [];
  for (const raw of value.lines) {
    const parsed = line(raw);
    if (!parsed) return null;
    lines.push(parsed);
  }

  const netMinor = int(totals.net_minor);
  const vatMinor = int(totals.vat_minor);
  const grossMinor = int(totals.gross_minor);
  if (netMinor === null || vatMinor === null || grossMinor === null || grossMinor !== netMinor + vatMinor) {
    return null;
  }
  const notes = value.notes === null ? null : str(value.notes);

  return {
    number,
    issueDate,
    supplyDate,
    dueDate,
    currency: "GBP",
    vatRegistered: vatNumber !== null,
    seller: { name: sellerName, address: sellerAddress, vatNumber, bank },
    recipient: { kind, name: recipientName, address: recipientAddress, email },
    lines,
    totals: { netMinor, vatMinor, grossMinor },
    notes,
  };
}
```

### Task 3: The four tables, the freeze, the arithmetic in SQL, and every census they trip

The tables arrive with RLS on, no client write grant, and their place in every catalogue the schema already keeps (audit actions, retention roll, export catalogue). Those catalogues go red the moment a table exists without them, so they ship in the same task. No RPC yet: Tasks 4–7 add them.

**Files:**
- Create: `supabase/migrations/20261106000000_invoice_tables.sql`
- Create: `supabase/tests/rls_invoices.sql`
- Create: `tests/unit/invoices/arithmetic-cases-match-sql.test.ts`
- Modify: `supabase/tests/run-rls-tests.sh` (run the suite; pins for L6, leakage, closure backup)
- Modify: `supabase/tests/rls_phase2_crm.sql:1287-1291` (L6: 34 → 36)
- Modify: `supabase/tests/network_leakage_phase6.sql` (IN10 after IN9, line ~1111)
- Modify: `supabase/tests/rls_phase8_closure_backup.sql` (`ck.seed`, KS1 37 → 41, housekeeping)
- Modify: `lib/admin/audit-actions.ts` (`PLATFORM_AUDIT_ACTIONS`)
- Modify: `tests/integration/security/phase7-permission-matrix.test.ts` (`PRE_PHASE7_RELATIONS`)
- Modify: `tests/integration/export/completeness-census.test.ts` (rolls, 57 → 61)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ARITHMETIC_CASES` (Task 2), `private.permitted_organisations(text)`, `private.erased_user_id()`, `private.set_updated_at()`.
- Produces (SQL): tables `public.invoice_settings`, `public.invoices`, `public.invoice_lines`, `public.invoice_sends` (columns as below); `private.invoice_line_amounts(p_quantity numeric, p_unit_price_minor bigint, p_vat_rate text) returns table (net_minor bigint, vat_minor bigint)`; `private.format_invoice_number(p_prefix text, p_n integer) returns text`; `private.build_invoice_snapshot(p_invoice_id uuid, p_number text, p_issue date, p_supply date, p_due date) returns jsonb`; index `invoices_org_created_id_idx` (Task 9's list sort); audit actions `invoice.issued`, `invoice.sent`, `invoice.marked_paid`, `invoice.voided`, `invoice.pdf_downloaded`, `invoice_settings.changed`, `invoice_settings.bank_details_changed`.
- Produces (psql): `supabase/tests/rls_invoices.sql` with schema `inv`, helpers `inv.val(label, sql, expected)` and `inv.record`, fixture variables (below) that later tasks reuse, and a `§ IZ HOUSEKEEPING` block that later tasks insert their sections **above**.
  Fixture psql variables: users `own_a adm_a fin_a mgr_a ro_a fin_b fin_s fin_f fin_p`; orgs `org_a` (pro) `org_b` (pro, no VAT number) `org_s` (starter) `org_f` (free) `org_p` (pro, purged in § IF); claims `own_a_c adm_a_c fin_a_c fin_b_c fin_s_c fin_f_c` (aal2) and `fin_a_c1 mgr_a_c ro_a_c` (aal1); records `com_a con_a ev_a fe_a com_b con_b con_s con_p`; invoices `inv_ad` (A draft, to `con_a`, on `ev_a`), `inv_ai` (A issued `FIX-0001`, to `com_a`), `inv_bd` (B draft), `inv_sd` (S draft), `inv_pi` (P issued).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the binding test (fails: the SQL file does not exist yet)**

```ts
// tests/unit/invoices/arithmetic-cases-match-sql.test.ts
// The SQL arithmetic is tested in supabase/tests/rls_invoices.sql § IA against
// its own copy of the case table. This binds that copy to the TypeScript one,
// row for row, so the two implementations are tested against ONE table.
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { ARITHMETIC_CASES } from "./arithmetic-cases";

const SQL = readFileSync(path.join(process.cwd(), "supabase", "tests", "rls_invoices.sql"), "utf8");

function sqlCases() {
  const block = SQL.split("-- ARITHMETIC CASES BEGIN")[1]?.split("-- ARITHMETIC CASES END")[0] ?? "";
  const row = /\('(\d+\.\d{2})',\s*(\d+),\s*'([a-z_0-9]+)',\s*(\d+),\s*(\d+)\)/g;
  return [...block.matchAll(row)].map((m) => ({
    quantity: m[1],
    unitPriceMinor: Number(m[2]),
    rate: m[3],
    netMinor: Number(m[4]),
    vatMinor: Number(m[5]),
  }));
}

describe("the SQL suite's arithmetic cases are the TypeScript ones", () => {
  it("finds the block at all, so this cannot pass vacuously", () => {
    expect(sqlCases().length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
  });
  it("matches row for row, in order", () => {
    expect(sqlCases()).toEqual(ARITHMETIC_CASES.map((c) => ({ ...c })));
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the psql suite's first half (header, helpers, fixtures, § IT § IA § IR § IB § IF § IN § IC § IZ)**

Create `supabase/tests/rls_invoices.sql`:
```sql
-- ===========================================================================
-- INVOICES, PART 1 OF 3 (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-invoices-design.md).
--
-- Task 3 (20261106000000_invoice_tables.sql):
--   IT  the four tables, their constraints, grants and policies
--   IA  the line/VAT arithmetic and the numbering (bound to the TS port)
--   IR  who reads an invoice, its lines, its sends and the settings
--   IB  breakout: no client role writes any of the four tables
--   IF  the freeze: an issued invoice's content cannot change, for any role
--   IN  the network views never read an invoice table
--   IC  the catalogues the tables joined (audit, retention, export)
-- Later tasks add their sections ABOVE § IZ.
--
-- EVERY ASSERTION IS ONE LINE BEGINNING `select inv.val(`: run-rls-tests.sh's
-- pin is `grep -c '^select inv.val(' supabase/tests/rls_invoices.sql`.
-- Re-runnable on one database: every fixture is a fresh uuid, § IZ removes them.
-- ===========================================================================
\set ON_ERROR_STOP on
\pset pager off

drop schema if exists inv cascade;
create schema inv;

create table inv.results (
  seq serial primary key, label text, expected text, actual text, pass boolean
);

create function inv.record(p_label text, p_expected text, p_actual text)
returns void language sql security definer set search_path = '' as $$
  insert into inv.results (label, expected, actual, pass)
  values (p_label, p_expected, p_actual, p_expected is not distinct from p_actual);
$$;

-- Runs p_sql and records its single text result, or `denied(<sqlstate>:<hint>)`
-- when it raises. A refusal is ALWAYS a recorded row, never a psql ERROR line
-- (psql exits 0 on ERROR; run_suite counts both, and ON_ERROR_STOP halts here).
create function inv.val(p_label text, p_sql text, p_expected text)
returns void language plpgsql security invoker set search_path = 'pg_catalog, public' as $$
declare v_actual text; v_hint text;
begin
  begin
    execute p_sql into v_actual;
    v_actual := coalesce(v_actual, '<null>');
  exception when others then
    get stacked diagnostics v_hint = pg_exception_hint;
    if v_hint like 'Grant the required privileges%' then v_hint := 'missing_grant'; end if;
    v_actual := 'denied(' || sqlstate || coalesce(':' || nullif(v_hint, ''), '') || ')';
  end;
  perform inv.record(p_label, p_expected, v_actual);
end $$;

grant usage on schema inv to authenticated, service_role;
grant execute on function inv.record(text,text,text) to authenticated, service_role;
grant execute on function inv.val(text,text,text)    to authenticated, service_role;
```
